# SP92_C13_150+151: changes against the two witnesses

Reviewed pages: 25 of 25. Counts are places, not changed tokens. A place may differ from both witnesses, so the C and H columns overlap. Confidence columns count each logged place once. Additions of doubt alone and same-reading macro differences are excluded. Resolved C doubt marks are logged with crop evidence, including cases where the first reading is confirmed; these may change certainty rather than the underlying word.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled continuation under (59), subscript of X: `\unsure{\widetilde J|J}`. Crossing hatch obscures the zone where an independent tilde would be; C doubt retained after full formula crop.
- (61), upper limit sign / M0661: `\unsure{\uline{\lim}|\varinjlim}`. Blind full-formula review favors the plain rule; a small endpoint return still permits an arrow, so M0661 stays undecided.
- (61), lower limit sign: `\unsure{\uline{\lim}|\varinjlim}`. Matching endpoint ambiguity after whole-formula rereading; added uncertainty only relative to C.
- Opening ideal condition, overwritten first index: `\unsure{i|\deleted{x}\inserted{i}}`. A detached high dot supports i, but crossing oblique strokes below it could preserve an old x. The sequence of strokes cannot be certified; the alternative concerns revision state rather than a different final index.
- Line after (60), crossed separator after j0: `\unsure{\ast|\ne}`. The small crossed body has more than the simple points supplied by both drafts. An asterisk-like or not-equal-like construction remains possible.
- (61), both relations in lower index chain: `\unsure{<|\leq}`. Compact wedges have thickened lower returns; an independent lower bar cannot be securely excluded. No expected flag inequality is imposed.

### Unresolved places

- right-margin struck word: `\unsure{disposition}`. The cancellation crosses one connected sequence beginning with a looped d and ending in the clearly dotted i/t/i/on sequence. A descending p and following round o are visible in the middle. The former division into d’où plus a second word is not supported. Disposition is the favoured reading, but the crossed middle letters remain uncertain.
- left-margin struck formula: `\deleted{\unsure{NB} $V_J^{\unsure{L}}\cap V_{J'}^{L'}=V_{J\cap J'}^{L\cap L'}$}`. On the existing crop rotated clockwise 60 degrees, raised L-like strokes occur above both first V terms and L cap L-prime above the last; the last lower line reads J cap J-prime. The second superscript has a separate prime. The initial NB and the first raised L remain partly covered and uncertain. The full superscripted formula, not a formula without superscripts, is present.
- after first L_0 subset L, before ens: `\unsure{l'ens|) l'ens}`. The long curved stroke may be the l of l’ens or a closing parenthesis, followed by a very compressed l’ens. No separate opening parenthesis is visible. This is added uncertainty only, not a settled departure from C.
- first element in definition of L_0: `\unsure{élém|élément}`. The short ending is a low horizontal join with a small terminal turn. It is shorter than the following full élément; the final ent cannot be separately certified.
- third element in lower-right note: `\unsure{élément|élémt}`. The ending is compressed into a rule and a final t-like upstroke. It may include ent or be the abbreviation mt; no stop is added.
- isolated final body fragment: `\unsure{de|donc}`. The initial looped d is clear. The following low connected strokes are too dense to distinguish e from onc securely. The blind alors has no supporting separate a/l sequence.

### Unresolved places

- b), idéal de I′: `{}'I'`. Both a preceding elevated stroke and a following prime are visible. The function of the left stroke is not settled.
- Cancelled existence wording after Sous ces conditions: `une`. The word under the strike is entangled with the lower replacement; plus alone is readable.
- Connecting word after unique,: `et`. The old and new layers intersect; neither the apostrophe nor the full sequence for c'est is separable.
- Inserted proof phrase I′ est donné: `donné`. The final sweep is compressed; one ending is visible but a separate extra e cannot confidently be excluded.
- Tiny formula reference in proof-opening insertion: `67`. The two-digit area is a dense ink blot within clear parentheses. Other on-page 67 references support the proposed reading without making the strokes legible.
- Punctuation after i≤j⇒i∈J: `..`. One clear stop and one smaller baseline mark are present; the smaller one may be incidental. A further tiny raised speck is not counted as a third stop.
- Cancelled old second word before définition: `par`. Its old baseline strokes are obscured by a heavy strike and the separate new par written above.

The remaining low letters lie under the cancellation; the detached stroke near the end may be an accent or i dot. - Reworked initial of proespace: `\unsure{proespace|\deleted{t}proespace}`. A tall stroke and transverse crossing at the initial may be reworking of p or an older t; the separate older letter cannot be certified. The rest of proespace is clear.

### Unresolved places

- NB M0680: `réduit`. Dense retracing obscures the middle letters; adjacent Et ça se and donc par are clearer.
- NB M0681: `cas`. The short word before dans (70) cannot be isolated confidently from the slanted continuation.
- NB punctuation after (70): `,`. No distinct comma can be separated reliably from the following small où and the skew of the line; retained narrowly uncertain.
- NB M0682: `inutile et peu`. Tall crossed strokes and compressed ending leave the phrase unconfirmed.
- NB M0683: `moins conclusif`. Flattened connected strokes and a final f-like descender support but do not certify these words.
- Cancelled clause after semi-simpliciaux: `résultent`. Cancellation obscures the word; a p-like descending stroke and tall l-like stroke keep H’s alternative possible.
- Cancelled opening: `les`. Repeated zigzags obscure the letter bodies; retains C doubt.
- Cancelled opening: `V_{ijk}`. The subscript strokes remain visible, but the base letter is heavily crossed; retains C doubt.
- Cancelled recollement: `recollement`. Initial rec and tall loops are compatible; compressed middle/end under the strike are not all plain.
- Inserted limit above recollement: `\varinjlim`. The right end of the bar is a downward terminal, not a separately distinguishable arrowhead. This is an added doubt only, not a witness-reading change row.
- Cancelled phrase above les flèches: `au lieu de`. The crossed line has three compact groups with one high l in the middle and a final clearly looped de. The longer flèches sequence and its f descender cannot be followed. Au lieu de is favoured, but crossed middle letters remain uncertain.
- Formula (72), M0685: `J\deleted{\}}`. The J is retraced and the separate doubled brace-like stroke has uncertain deletion state; whole formula reviewed.
- (73), outer closing parenthesis at the cropped right edge: `)\unsure{)}`. The small closing parenthesis immediately after F is visible. A longer separate lower diagonal reaches the right border below it, but the remainder of the outer closing curve is clipped. Mark the partial outer character rather than silently treating it as fully visible.

### Unresolved places

- Top diagonal note, cancelled symbol after non: `\deleted{\unsure{\Sigma_i}}`. The compact underlying angular shape and an i-like dot survive, but cancellation prevents certification of the complete Sigma_i.
- Top diagonal note, M0688: `\unsure{idem pour}`. The dotted initial, taller stem and p-like descender are partly visible; crossing darker ink prevents separation of all letters.
- Lower correction under (70), first old superscript: `X_J^{\deleted{\unsure{*}}}`. The dense scribble is certainly cancelled and confined to the raised position, but the original star's individual branches are not all independently visible.
- Lower correction under (70), second old superscript: `X_J^{\deleted{\unsure{*}}}`. A distinct cancellation covers the second raised position; a star-like residual form is retained without a guessed resolution.
- (72), cancelled glyph before the inner opening parenthesis: `\deleted{\unsure{b}}`. The loop and upright are b-like, but dense retracing obscures the original complete character.
- Left NB at (72), M0692: `\unsure{l'on déduit}`. Crossing main-line ink obscures the group after que; the short word before the tuple is also not secure.
- Cancelled prelude before (73), final group: `\deleted{Par suite \unsure{d'un}}`. Par suite is legible; the last group's apostrophe and u/n separation cannot be certified under the strike.
- (74), final condition, M0693: `\unsure{avec|ayant}`. The low a/v/e/c-like run now favours avec; the blind reading also chose it. The central retrace remains too dense to certify every join or exclude ayant. Reverse the preferred order only, and retain the doubt.
- (70), first difference-shaped sign: `\unsure{\setminus|>}`. Two oblique arms meet in a wedge-like outline. The expected set difference does not settle whether both arms are intentional; retain the local alternative.
- Left X note, relation M0690: `\unsure{=|\stackrel{?}{=}}`. Two equals bars are separate from a tall curled overtrace. The overtrace is not a third equals bar, but may qualify the relation; the former complete resolution is withdrawn.
- Left NB at (72), word before tuple: `\unsure{aux|des}`. The long stratification has the f descender and t stems, and type J with its comma is plain. Que is readable before the crossed group; the latter still cannot be separated securely into letters. Before the tuple there is a low oval, low joins and an angular final return, favouring aux; no clear d ascender can be certified. Keep a local aux/des doubt instead of the earlier certain des.
- Left NB at (72), word after tuple: `\unsure{avec|où}`. The short group after the tuple has a small low opening and joined strokes with a separate upper mark. It does not securely distinguish avec from the blind où; the earlier certainty is withdrawn.
- Left NB at (72), opening final condition sign: `\unsure{\forall|j,}`. The second letter is an h with tall stem and one shoulder; the final I has two transverse bars. The opening sign has a long right stem, a lower angled left arm and a short transverse return, favouring forall, but the clipped left edge prevents excluding j with punctuation. There is no secure isolated j-dot or comma. Retain the two readings locally.

### Unresolved places

- Upper marginal prose after J prec L: `en fait on`. The three reduced word groups remain uncertain where the slanted note and main-line writing meet; the syntax is not completed by inference.
- Canceled T definition after definitional equality: `T_J\cap\Sigma_J`. Cancellation obscures several linking strokes, so the whole short former expression remains marked unsure.
- Decoration above final T_j in first T definition: `\unsure{T_j^{\deleted{\circ}}|\dot T_j}`. The raised blot has no independent horizontal bar; a canceled circle and a dot cannot be separated under its dense retrace.
- Canceled word after final in second marginal limit: `est`. Low initial movements and taller final stem resemble the live est above, but the cancellation prevents certainty.
- Opening margin, M0694 reduced final group: `\unsure{très sûr|sûr}`. Je ne suis pas is readable. The small following group is partly crossed by the large initial downstroke from the preceding line; sûr is supported, but separating the proposed très from it is not secure. Retain a local très sûr/sûr doubt.
- Lower diagram, decoration of the middle T: `\unsure{\mathring T_{I'_0}|\dot T_{I'_0}}`. The middle raised mark is almost filled, unlike the two open circles at either side. A minute pale centre may remain, but it does not securely decide ring versus dot.

### Unresolved places

- (75), cancelled character before j: `\deleted{\unsure{j}}`. The additional character and its deletion are clear, but overlapping strokes obscure its complete letter form.
- (75), live barred capital under second union: `\unsure{\bar I|\bar J}`. The internal stem and bilateral bars favor I; lower-bar retracing leaves J possible. The changed first reading is logged in C22 while the alternative remains explicit.
- (76), cancelled sign before Sigma_{i_0,i_1,i_2}: `\unsure{\cap}`. The distinct sign is crossed densely. C’s struck doubt is not removed.
- (76), three lower upward marks: `\unsure{\uparrow|\rotatebox{-90}{$\in$}}`. Each sign has an outer arch and a central stroke; these can be either an upward arrow with curved head or quarter-turned membership. Cancellation prevents resolving their construction, so both readings are explicit.
- (76), separately cancelled exponent in right-hand note: `\deleted{\unsure{I'-J|I'\setminus J}}`. The crossed superscript extends across three groups: a tall I-like form with a separate prime, an intervening stroke, and a right-hand J with a lower hook. It is wider than one flat/natural glyph. The middle separator remains obscured; retain I-prime minus/slash J alternatives under the deletion.
- (77), leading Sigma exponent: `\unsure{\flat|\natural|\deleted{\flat}|\deleted{\natural}}`. Low bowl and crossing upper strokes do not resolve glyph or state; repetition in (78)–(79) does not separate original strokes from cancellation.
- (77), cancelled character before the inner-union j: `\deleted{\unsure{\forall}}`. The dense crossing leaves too little unambiguous original structure to resolve C’s struck doubt.
- (78), leading Sigma exponent: `\unsure{\flat|\natural|\deleted{\flat}|\deleted{\natural}}`. Same recurring bowl/crossing pattern as (77); deletion state remains open.
- (79), upper-left Sigma exponent, M0704: `\unsure{\flat|\natural|\deleted{\flat}|\deleted{\natural}}`. The complete diagram and tight crop leave the flat/natural choice and possible cancellation undecided.
- (79), small T decoration in the vertical-arrow label: `\unsure{T_{\Sigma_J\Sigma_{I'}}^*|\dot T_{\Sigma_J\Sigma_{I'}}}`. The tiny raised mark has several angular projections, unlike the round filled dots in75–78, but its size and retracing prevent distinguishing a star from a rough dot with certainty. The subscripts have no separating comma.

### Unresolved places

- (80), first subscript in right-hand i condition: `i_{\unsure{q|0}}`. The loop and descending right return favour q; the heavy small form still leaves0 possible. The i bases and two horizontal continuation rules are clear.
- Canceled word after et below (80): `\deleted{\unsure{ensuite}}`. A candidate word remains visible, but the rising strike prevents every letter from being read; the C doubt stays.
- Inserted-then-canceled short phrase above côniques: `\inserted{\deleted{\unsure{de ri.}}}`. Small letter joins and possible terminal stop merge with the strike. No expansion is supplied and the C doubt is retained.
- (80), first j subscript in the parenthesized flag, M0705: `j_{\unsure{1|q}}`. The small upright has a heavily retraced cap with a minute loop-like opening. Its shape does not securely exclude q; restore local uncertainty.

### Unresolved places

- heading, third V index: `V_{\unsure{\Sigma|J|\Delta}}`. A top horizontal cap, oblique middle and broad curved lower return favour Sigma; retracing closes part of the form and leaves J/Delta alternatives. The adjacent clean J is differently shaped. No underlying cancellation is separately recoverable.
- cancelled ens. after forment une: `\deleted{\unsure{ens.|cat.}}`. The low opening joins lie under the heavy strike; a raised terminal remnant also admits cat. Neither reading has all its letters independently clear.
- overwritten intersections after stable par: `\unsure{intersecti-|intersections}`. The terminal i-like stroke, short rule and following comma are visible. The dense overwritten interior prevents a secure full-word reading; no completion is silently supplied.
- tail of cancelled Soit L draft: `\unsure{on a}`. A round opening and a-like ending survive under the strike; the intervening joining strokes are not separately legible.
- cancelled opening before Soit: `\unsure{\S|\deleted{Si}|\deleted{L}}`. The isolated opening has two vertically overlapping loop/curve groups and a long lower return. No independent i dot or separate cancelling stroke is secure; a section sign and an overtraced abandoned letter remain alternatives.
- cancelled character after opposé à: `\deleted{$\unsure{I}$}`. Upper and lower bars suggest I, but extra diagonal strokes obscure the central form. The cancelled state is firmer than the letter.
- cancelled membership before i≥l: `\deleted{\unsure{l\in I|i\in I}}`. The first letter is cut by the diagonal strike and the final capital is overtraced; the visible membership sign does not settle either endpoint.
- cancelled Donc before Ainsi: `\deleted{\unsure{Donc}}`. The opening D curve survives, while the interior letters sit under the broad cancellation.
- Top. associée, ending before à: `\unsure{associé|associée}`. The final accented stroke and small terminal return can represent a single é; an additional e cannot be isolated securely. Keep the agreement unresolved.

### Unresolved places

- Old phrase after C'est: `\deleted{\unsure{une propriété|une prop.|une top.}}`. Strike hides most joins and some uprights. Live replacement is separable, old text is not fully legible.
- First term of canceled added mathematics: `\deleted{\unsure{\bar J}\cap\widetilde J}`. Later intersection and tilde-J are clear, first barred glyph is heavily overwritten.
- Canceled bandes in Soient sentence: `\deleted{\unsure{bandes}}`. Tall b and ending support the candidate, but every middle letter is not independently plain.
- Canceled l'inclusion: `\deleted{\unsure{inclusion|l'inclusion}}`. Low run is obscured by strike and overlaps the live replacement.
- First canceled lower et: `\deleted{\unsure{et|\ell|\xi}}`. Several looped and horizontal strokes overlap. The former two-letter et and a single crossed looped sign are both possible.
- First lower I\prime relation: `I'\unsure{=|\subset}\bar K`. The two near-parallel strokes resemble the linked prose equals sign below, but their curved left junction still admits subset. No mathematical correction is made.
- Second canceled lower et: `\deleted{\unsure{et|donc}}`. The initial loop and upright survive, followed by a long thick low run; that run may contain further letters, so donc is not excluded.
- Right delimiter of number87: `(87\unsure{)|]}`. Nearly straight downstroke and lower-left turn; no decisive upper bracket arm. Added doubt only.
- Opening word after aussi: `\unsure{donc|alors}`. Tall opening stroke and compact ending favour donc; the pen joins do not securely exclude a plus l of alors.
- Inserted hypothesis, word before J prec K: `\unsure{avec|on a}`. The tiny low groups are crossed by dense joins; the opening dot/loop and later short return do not securely separate a single avec from on a.
- K after enfin and canceled short sign: `\unsure{K|K\prime}`. Two minute upper-right traces are visible near K, but no continuous prime stroke is secure; preserve the uncertainty.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled opening noun: `\deleted{\unsure{filtration}}`. The initial tall f and following looped l rule out a strat- opening; the broad strike still hides some lower joins, so the word remains locally doubtful.
- Raised and cancelled opening adjective: `\inserted{\deleted{\unsure{indicée|indicielle|induite}}}`. The tiny raised word has an initial dotted short stroke and a later tall stem, but its low joins are canceled. Indicée is favored without certifying the full canceled word from the live heading.
- Strat 1 adjective: `\unsure{fini|finie}`. The dotted final i is clear; a low trailing curl can be either a separate final e or the terminal return of i. Enclosing insertion leaders do not resolve it. Decision M0718 remains undecided.
- Cancelled pre-flag phrase: `\deleted{\unsure{Posons les conditions|Pour ces conditions}}`. Surviving round bodies favor Posons and a tall l favors les, so the first reading changes, but the crossing hides parts of the joined letters and conditions. Change C11 records only the favored first reading.
- Canceled word before live coproduct: `\deleted{\unsure{les}}`. The looped l and low e/s-like remnants are under the left crossing stroke. The two following uprights and bottom bar are separately live coproduct ink, and its lower index is live.
- Stop after cancelled syst: `\deleted{syst\unsure{.|}}`. The letters s-y-s-t are individually readable. No separate stop is visible, but the heavy horizontal strike passes through the position where one could have been written; uncertainty is limited to punctuation.
- Cancelled large after semi-simplicial: `\deleted{\unsure{large}}`. The tall l and g descender support large, while the crossed a/r/e do not meet the all-letters-plain threshold. The following separate live au sens large is fully readable.
- First starred-space index in final inline math: `X_{\unsure{i|d}}^*`. A detached dot supports i, but the left bowl, tall stem and right foot resemble the adjacent d, unlike the short dotted i in the final i_0. The whole group was read and remains unresolved; adding this doubt alone does not count as a change.
- Strat3, compressed group before intersection formula: `\unsure{on a|$\mathord{\cdot}\mathord{-}\mathord{\cdot}$}`. Only three very small low marks remain: point, horizontal stroke, point. They may be the compressed o/n/a bodies used elsewhere, but distinct letter contours are not secure.

### Unresolved places

- (90), cancelled group: `\deleted{\unsure{\bigcup|X_j}}`. The outer contour has two uprights and a rounded lower join, favoring a union sign. Internal diagonal cancellation also permits the earlier X/index reconstruction; the old sign is not certified.
- restriction paragraph, cancelled article: `\deleted{\unsure{la|r}}`. A small upright and lower loop remain under two diagonal strokes; neither the full article nor a single r-like abandoned start can be certified.
- cancelled clause, opening: `\unsure{Cet axiome|Stratifications}`. The capital-like initial and separate tall t-like stroke favor Cet before the longer low group; the broad cancellation still prevents certification of every letter.
- cancelled clause, terminal word: `\unsure{impliq|implique}`. The visible ending is q-like with a descending stroke, but the cancellation merges with the terminal traces. The written extent cannot be certified or silently expanded.
- last line, on aura: `\unsure{on aura}`. The very low compressed o/n and vowel joins make the phrase less secure than the neighboring donc, si and membership symbols; H's narrow doubt is retained.
- Margin, implication after X_i not equal phi: `\unsure{\Rightarrow|\rightarrow}`. The right head is visible, but the short shaft is a filled retraced block with no continuously separable pair of lines. Keep single/double-shaft uncertainty.

### Unresolved places

- (95), relation between starred X_J and X_J: `\unsure{\in|\subset}`. A separate-looking middle level favors membership, but thick lower retracing could be a repeated inclusion curve. This is a newly added doubt, not a settled correction.
- Canceled expression after donc: `\unsure{\overline{X_J^*}=X_J^*}`. The cancellation covers the middle relation and the second subscript; not every stroke can be separated. Retains C’s provisional reading, unresolved.
- Canceled letter after final Si: `\unsure{A|B}`. Top and bottom crossings overlap a tall left side and right loops; neither capital is safe enough to resolve the canceled ink.
- After Si on a (Strat5): `\unsure{alors|les}`. A large looped tall stroke and two low groups can encode compressed alors, but the opening a and internal r cannot be isolated securely from l/e/s. Keep the local word unresolved.

### Unresolved places

- (92), closing delimiter: `(92\unsure{)}`. The right curved stroke is connected to the digit's preceding rising stroke, so an independent delimiter cannot be certified. The whole numbered formula is assigned to a blind box.
- Cancelled baseline noun: `\deleted{\unsure{ens.|fam.}}`. The first loop and low humps are crossed; the outline does not decide between these short forms.
- M0721, replacement noun: `\unsure{rec.|rec}`. The tiny initial shoulder, middle loop and c-like return suggest rec but the boundaries remain weak; there is no independent stop that can be certified.
- NB mapping endpoint: `\unsure{\mapsto|\mathrel{\mapstochar\relbar}}`. A left tailbar and horizontal shaft are clear. The right end curls upward and thickens, but separate arrowhead arms cannot be securely distinguished from a terminal pen return.
- Cancelled paragraph, opening capital and pronoun: `\unsure{Nous|On}`. The angular lower group is on the prose baseline but intersects the NB border. Following rounded and low strokes can be read ous; their exact separation remains uncertain. No independent deleted mathematical term is supplied from the same ink.
- Cancelled paragraph, verb ending: `\unsure{utilisera|utiliserons}`. The beginning is clear, while the final low strokes merge with the cancellation. Agreement with the preceding pronoun is not used as evidence.
- Cancelled paragraph, noun: `\unsure{notation|notations}`. The t ascenders and i dot survive; the initial and final low strokes are merged with the long strike, leaving the C doubt unresolved.
- Cancelled paragraph, adverb: `\unsure{surtout}`. The low sur-like beginning and tall crossed t support surtout, but not all intervening and final letters are independently visible.
- Opening second implication, low group: `\unsure{\text{on a}|\mathord{\cdot}\mathord{-}\mathord{\cdot}}`. Three low separated marks can be a compressed on a or the literal point-rule-point group; no complete letter boundaries certify either.
- Last word before92: `\unsure{donc|même}`. The low beginning and long middle run permit donc; the possible m humps and absence of a clear independent ascender also permit même. No detached circumflex is certain.
- Inserted local finiteness ending: `\unsure{fini|finie}`. Two i dots are visible, but the final small return and curved leader overlap, preventing a secure decision on e.
- Condition before the unfinished paragraph: `\unsure{veut|est}`. The low opening loop and long fused middle are too compressed to separate v/e/u from e/s. The final crossed t is clear.
- Canceled final verb: `\unsure{aura|va}`. Cancellation covers the low internal strokes; the rounded terminal return does not certify all letters.

### Unresolved places

- Old word under réciproque: `\deleted{\illegible{1}}`. Thick cancellation leaves disconnected traces but not all letters; no lexical reconstruction is adopted.
- Cancelled opening above Soit: `\deleted{\unsure{Sous les|Sur les|donc les}}`. The crossed low run cannot distinguish these short forms; no full independent t certifies Soient.
- Cancelled numeral below first (95): `\deleted{\unsure{$96$|Or|Où}}`. Looping residues under the intersecting strokes permit a number or short word; neither is certain.
- Cancelled phrase before live on a: `\deleted{cela \unsure{revient|veut|vaut}}`. Cela has separately exposed c/e/l/a bodies; the second word is still partly hidden by the strike.
- Cancelled word after (i,j): `\deleted{\unsure{comme}}`. The low outline and initial c support the retained hypothesis, but crossing obscures the middle letters.
- Lower forward-arrow caption, after donc: `\unsure{tt|tte}`. Two crossed t stems are clear. The small final return could be a foot or a separate e. Added uncertainty alone does not count as a change against C; H’s different caption words are separately collated.
- Superscript of the marginal hook source: `\unsure{*|\prime}`. The thick oblique mark has a slight side protrusion, compatible with a compact star, but the separate crossing arms are not secure enough to exclude a prime.
- After et considérons formula: `\unsure{on a|$\mathord{\cdot}\mathord{-}\mathord{\cdot}$}`. Three tiny marks are compatible with compressed on a and with a point-rule-point group; retain the local ambiguity.
- After canceled cela phrase: `\unsure{on a|$\mathord{\cdot}\mathord{-}\mathord{\cdot}$}`. The low isolated dot, horizontal run and last dot do not independently identify complete letters.
- Before d'être équivalente: `\unsure{chance|chances}`. A low final return may be the last e finish or a compressed s; the beginning chance is plain.

### Unresolved places

- Canceled verb before first aura: `trouve`. A strong horizontal strike obscures the interior letters. The C proposal is retained under uncertainty; only the cancellation state is settled.
- Canceled V term after enfin: `V_{\unsure{ij|j}}`. The large curved V is clear; its lower j hook survives but the possible i is inside the cancellation.
- Cancelled old start before comme/aura: `\unsure{d|donc|On}`. The compact old loop and basal return remain crossed; no complete internal word boundaries are secure.
- Opening line at right paper edge: `incomparab\unsure{l}`. The end of the b/l group meets the paper boundary. Final es is absent from the surviving image and is not completed.
- End of line containing il suffit: `d'introduir`. The word’s terminal return reaches the black edge; no final e is supplied outside the surviving writing.
- Before pour éviter: `(`. The long curved stroke overlaps the descending final p from the slanted marginal protopos; its status as a separate main-text delimiter remains uncertain.
- Before X metrisable: `\unsure{et|tt}`. Two linked uprights and a common bottom return permit compressed et or tt.
- Live word after the canceled old start: `\unsure{comme|aura}`. The low linked run lacks independently separable internal letters; its final return can belong to e or a.

### Unresolved places

- (99) parenthetical connector: `\unsure{\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\relbar}|\hookrightarrow}`. The endpoint is an upward blunt nub, unlike clearly separated arrowhead arms elsewhere. Headless is ink-favored, but an abbreviated head cannot be excluded.
- Cancelled old word group before X_i_n*: `\deleted{\illegible{1} \unsure{du|de}}`. The left group remains unreadable. On the right, d is exposed; the low crossed ending favors u but permits e. Neither complete right-hand word is certified.
- First X in raised parenthesis: `\unsure{X_{i_n}|X_{i_n}^*}`. No star can be isolated independently of the adjacent crowded label, but the overlap prevents ruling it out absolutely.
- First raised connector label, M0728: `\unsure{\overset h{(\sim)}}`. Parenthesis curves and h/tilde-like strokes remain entangled with a descender from the preceding line. The connector body itself is a visible plain shaft.
- (104), first X superscript: `X_J^{\unsure{h|*}}`. The small filled glyph is h-shaped, with an upright and right branches, but a compressed star remains possible. Nearby stars have a different radial construction.
- After raised question-mark alors: `\unsure{on a|$\mathord{\cdot}\mathord{-}\mathord{\cdot}$}`. A low dark point, longer horizontal run and final point can encode compressed on a or the literal three marks. Their letter boundaries are not secure.

### Unresolved places

- (105), lower limit stroke: `\unsure{\uline{\lim}|\varinjlim}`. A tiny downturned right finish does not certify an arrowhead. This is a newly added uncertainty, not a settled reading change.
- cancelled word below (105): `\deleted{\unsure{et|Et}}`. The crossed looping body does not securely distinguish uppercase E from lowercase e.
- (106), cancelled superscript after live L: `\deleted{\unsure{\flat|\natural}}`. Heavy scribble prevents separating the angular original glyph.
- (106), M0730 relation: `\unsure{\subset|=}`. Joined left return admits subset or connected equality; expected equality does not decide ink.
- (106), old overwritten superscript: `\deleted{\unsure{Z}}`. The overlying X_L^* merges with the old horizontals and diagonal; the old Z cannot be certified letter by letter.
- Suggestion, first superscript M0731: `\unsure{\flat|\natural}`. The angular bowl and second tail admit both proposed signs.
- Suggestion, second superscript M0732: `\unsure{\flat|\natural}`. Same unresolved angular form as the first proposed superscript.
- cancelled central limit, index: `\unsure{d\in\mathrm{Drap}_{\geq2}(J\cup L)}`. Several oblique strikes merge with Drap and the small comparison/2 index; the complete old index remains qualified.
- NB, noun at page edge: `drapea\unsure{u}`. The final short body may be u; no independent x is visible before the paper boundary. Nothing is completed past the edge.
- NB, overwritten middle flag group: `\unsure{\mathord{-}i_\beta\cdot|\mathord{-}\deleted{i_\beta}\cdot|\cdots}`. A curled upper body and beta-like lower double loop are present between a short bar and point. Revision state is not separable from retracing.
- NB, word before first i_alpha: `\unsure{les|tous}`. Tall loop favors l, but the low bodies are densely retraced and do not securely exclude tous.
- starred footnote, initial La/la: `\unsure{La|la}`. The tall opening l-form does not clearly distinguish case.
- starred footnote, cancelled word before est: `\deleted{\unsure{la}}`. The l-like loop survives but the next letter is partly hidden beneath the strike.
- Word before notation102: `\unsure{avec|en}`. The small low run does not expose enough separate letter bodies to choose between the two short words.
- Suggestion, group before lieu/lien: `\unsure{au|$\mathord{-}$}`. A short horizontal mark with a thick left return may be compressed au or a literal rule.
- Suggestion, word before de: `\unsure{lieu|lien}`. Tall l, dotted i and the following low connected ending are clear, but the final u/n return is merged.

### Unresolved places

- marginal canceled group, final relation only: `X_J^*\unsure{\cap|\cup}`. Cancellation obscures the direction of the final arch; X, J and star are visible.
- main canceled group, star and final relation: `X_J^{\unsure{*}}\unsure{\cap|\cup}`. The upper star-like crossing and final arch overlap cancellation; X and J remain visible.
- (110), target first subscript: `\unsure{J|\square}`. Dark top/right/bottom resembles J, but a lighter left vertical closes the contour.
- (111), detached mark after initial J: `J\unsure{,|0}`. The isolated curled mark has an upper dark lobe and long downward return; its presence is clear but identity/attachment uncertain.
- 109 limit lower stroke: `\unsure{\uline{\lim}|\varinjlim}`. The endpoint is a small knob without securely separable arrow arms; a headed limit is not certified.
- Canceled head word: `\deleted{\unsure{clairement|claire et|claire est}}`. The crossed middle does not expose all letters or word boundaries.
- Label preceding ordered bands: `\unsure{112|111}`. The terminal digit is compressed and its lower turn cannot securely establish2; the expected sequence is not substituted for ink.
- Inverted canceled block, upper row: `\unsure{V_J^L}\,\unsure{=}\,\bigcup_{\unsure{j\in J}}V_j\cap X_L^*=`. The right X_L* and terminal equality are clear; cancellations cross the opening body, relation and lower index.
- Inverted canceled block, middle row: `\bigcup_{\unsure{l\in L}}{\unsure{V}}_{\unsure{l|L}}^{\unsure{Z|2}}`. The U-shaped union is clear, while crossed index strokes and the following V-like body remain provisional.
- Inverted canceled block, lower row: `\unsure{\supseteq|\geq}\,\unsure{Z|2}=X_L^*`. A right-facing curved/angular return lies above a short lower rule; the following equals and X_L* are clear.

### Unresolved places

- Cancelled upper arrow labels: `\unsure{\delta_1},\unsure{\delta_2}`. Hatching obscures the first two label joins and indices. The exposed lowest delta3 is clear.
- Upper-right lower product: `\unsure{\sigma|\delta}_3\delta_3`. Top bar and low closed loop favor sigma; dense retracing leaves a delta-like spine. No certainty beyond that glyph.
- First left p subscript: `p_{\unsure{1|0}}`. Saturated rounded numeral has a short descending right tail, differing from the thin1 and open0 in nearby terms. Added uncertainty alone does not create a change row.
- Cancelled old projection subscript: `\deleted{\unsure{21}}`. Strike traverses the old pair; raised replacement23 is clear. Old reading remains unconfirmed.
- Small raised terminal in third delta-rho identity: `{}^{\unsure{a|\leftarrow}}`. Raised loop/return and angled left stroke do not securely distinguish a from a leftward arrow.
- Cancelled initial a-like glyph: `\deleted{\unsure{a}}`. Loop and descending return survive, but cancellation prevents full identification.
- Alleged comma beside first identity box: `no independent comma`. Nearby downstroke belongs to cancelled p draft or box junction; no independent punctuation can be separated. Omission is preferred without introducing an extra mark.
- Cancelled expression beside identity box: `\deleted{p_{\unsure{1|12}}\delta_{\unsure{1|0}}}`. Crossings hide the p index length; the exposed low delta index favors an upright1, but0 cannot be excluded.
- Cancelled raised annotation after rho: `\rho^{\deleted{\unsure{2|\sigma}}}\delta_1`. Small raised contour is independently crossed and remains2/sigma.
- Cancelled underlabel below main delta1: `\deleted{\unsure{\rho}}`. The loop and stem are largely overwritten by short crossing strokes; no new certainty.
- Cancelled inequality RHS index: `\unsure{p_{22}|p_{12}|p_{23}}`. Angled first entry with hooked foot favors2, second digit favors2; the strike leaves12/23 possible. No algebraic expectation decides it.
- New cancelled subscript below rho2: `\rho_{\deleted{\unsure{1}}}^{2}`. Separate upright crossed by two transverse marks is certain as a deletion; exact numeral is tentative.
- Cancelled Ens-like label: `\deleted{$\unsure{(\mathrm{Ens})|\mathrm{ens}}$}`. Opening parenthesis-like trace and E/e, n, s remnants are crossed; both parentheses/case not certified.
- Cancelled lower group/index: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathcal G|\mathfrak S}_{\unsure{2|1}}}`. Left glyph is inseparable from crossing, while long diagonal and low rightward foot favor index2;1 remains possible.
- Bottom cancelled sigma-like glyph: `\deleted{\unsure{\sigma}}`. Dense crossed oval and top bar support only a tentative sigma; neighbouring rho2 stays live.
- Bottom left underlabel: `p_{\unsure{2|1}}`. Bent upper stroke, oblique descent and rightward foot favor2, but the paper edge clips the lowest tip.
- Canceled projection equality, first index: `p_{\unsure{12}}`. The thick crossing conceals independent boundaries of both small index digits.
- Bottom formula relation: `\unsure{=|\ne}`. Two horizontal bars survive; a dark connecting/retraced mark above them does not securely establish or exclude a crossing stroke.

### Changes after the blind second reading

- autograph, M0744: `\illegible{1}`. The tall loop, rounded middle and narrow turns are merged with an extended flourish; a familiar surname from the letterhead would be a conjecture.
- separate clipped lower line, M0745: `\illegible{1}`. Only stroke tops survive; the character bodies are beyond the image edge. No missing letters are supplied.

### Changes after the blind second reading

No reading changed after image adjudication.

### Unresolved places

- Small cancelled line inside the upper inventory: `\deleted{\unsure{\sigma p_{12}\quad\sigma p}}`. The sigma-like loops remain crossed and merged; p12 is partly clear, and the last p has no visible index. No full struck reading is certified.
- Cancelled supra-line draft above the middle central relation: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{id}}}`. The repeated cancellation merges both proposed letter bodies; not every letter is plainly separable.
- Six-p oval, upper-right contour: `\unsure{3}`. The two-bend mark is attached to the outline and resembles the other oval's curled closure; an exponent is not certified. It is positioned on the outline, not asserted as an exponent.
- Underlabel in final boxed product relation: `\unsure{q_1|p_1}`. A broad left oval and a smaller right bowl meet a heavily retraced stem. The final versus underlying letter cannot be separated.
- Detached mark above main-diagram E2: `\overset{\unsure{\prime|\cdot}}{E}_2`. A distinct dark short upright is present above E; its exact identity is not settled.
- Canceled p-pair separator: `p_0\unsure{,|/}p_1`. A short curved stroke survives within the longer crossing return, so comma is preferred but slash is not excluded.

| C01: lower name line at the original upper image edge, below the autograph in upright orientation | \unsure{ROUX} | — | \illegible{1} | Four separated character-top groups survive: a squared upper bowl, a rounded upper bowl, two upright strokes, then two sloping strokes. ROUX is compatible with these tops, but the first character’s distinguishing lower leg, the third character’s base and the final crossing are outside the image. No complete four-letter reading can be certified from this scan. The fragment is kept separately without completing its missing letter bodies. [crops/151_scan006_clipped_name.jxl](crops/151_scan006_clipped_name.jxl), original-pixel box (335, 0, 475, 45). | high |

### Unresolved places

- autograph, M0752: `\illegible{1}`. Tall loops and compressed turns overlap an extended flourish; the typed sender name cannot establish the signature’s letters.
- edge-clipped lower line, M0753: `\illegible{1}`. The character bodies needed to distinguish the first, third and fourth letters lie beyond the image edge.

### Changes after the blind second reading

No reading changed after image adjudication.

### Unresolved places

- Middle coordinate of the lower repeated left tuple: `(x,\unsure{x|z},y)`. The crossed middle letter has intersecting strokes compatible with x and a low angular return compatible with z; neither is certified.
- Separate deleted opening: `\deleted{\unsure{Donc.|On a.}}`. Horizontal cancellation merges the small internal letters; the capital loop and stop survive.
- Final determiner in deleted delta1 phrase: `\deleted{respecte \unsure{les|le}}`. The final return merges with the deletion line; a distinct final s cannot be certified.
- Right-edge respectent ending: `respect\unsure{ent}`. Visible e-like loop favors ent over ant; terminal arch and return touch the paper edge.
- Initial subscript and center of the cancelled right repeated relation: `\deleted{p_{\unsure{12}}\sigma_3\unsure{=\sigma}p_{12}}`. The first 12 subscript and central equals/sigma remain entangled with hatching; the sigma3 and final p12 are exposed.
- Separate canceled id-like entry: `\deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{id}}}`. The upright/loop remains merged with dense hatching; no full recovery claimed.
- Separate upper tuple: `(\unsure{x},\unsure{x|z},y)`. The first coordinate meets a diagonal cancellation; the middle intersecting/angled strokes admit x or z.
- First tuple comma after x: `\delta_1\sigma=(x\unsure{,}y,y)`. A separate comma cannot be distinguished securely from the first x terminal curl.
- Small-compartment caption word and deletion scope: `\unsure{application|\deleted{appl} constante}`. The heavy horizontal and compressed succeeding letters do not settle whether this is one live word or a canceled short form followed by another word.
- Small-compartment caption final subscript: `e_{\unsure{1|q}}`. The lower frame crosses the small subscript and its descending return.
